     __________________________________________________________________

           Title: S. Aurelii Augustini De Doctrina Christiana Libri
                  Quatuor et Enchiridion ad Laurentium
      Creator(s): Augustine of Hippo (Latin original, De Doctrina
                  Christiana begun 397, Book IV and the rest finished
                  426/7; Enchiridion c. 421-423)
                  Ex Benedictinorum recensione (the Maurist Benedictine
                  text) recognitos edidit Carolus Hermannus Bruder
                  (Editor)
     Print Basis: Editio stereotypa. Lipsiae [Leipzig]: Sumtibus et
                  Typis Caroli Tauchnitii, 1838.
          Rights: Public Domain
    Rights basis: 1838 publication date places this volume long out
                  of US copyright; no ambiguity.
    Language: lat
        Supplied: pulled directly from archive.org (item
                  dedoctrinachrist00augu, a Wellesley College scan) by
                  a Fable research agent for a sibling research session
                  with working network access
                  (session_01WLxhNbVhjkf1R2SAh8dxxT), 2026-09-08, for
                  the Latin Pastoral-Congregational Christianity world
                  build. This world's own corpus map had assigned De
                  Doctrina Christiana from the English NPNF102
                  translation only; no original-language witness
                  existed prior to this file. NOTE ON EDITION TYPE,
                  stated directly rather than left to look like a
                  critical edition: this is a 19th-century Tauchnitz
                  reprint of the 17th-century Maurist Benedictine text,
                  lightly revised by Bruder -- NOT a modern critical
                  edition. CSEL 80 (Green, ed., 1963) is the modern
                  critical edition but is confirmed IN COPYRIGHT
                  (verso reads "(C) HOELDER-PICHLER-TEMPSKY KG., WIEN
                  1963") and must never be vendored. A secondary PD
                  witness, Migne Patrologia Latina vol. 34 (1861,
                  archive.org item
                  patrologiae_cursus_completus_lat_vol_034), was also
                  checked and confirmed to carry this text (running
                  heads "DE DOCTRINA CHRISTIANA"), useful chiefly for
                  its PL column-number citation scheme, not vendored
                  here.
    Content note: Verified this pass: title page and preface date
                  (Feb. 1838) confirmed. Book I argumentum present;
                  Book IV's own opening line confirmed (quoted here in
                  normalized spelling -- the file's own raw OCR reads
                  "JlIoc opus nostnim" for "Hoc opus nostrum," not
                  corrected in the vendored text itself): "Hoc opus
                  nostrum, quod inscribitur de doctrina...". PRECISE
                  INTERNAL BOUNDARY, added 2026-09-08 (independent Opus
                  adversarial review, closing a gap this header
                  originally left only approximate): De Doctrina
                  Christiana runs from this file's own line 71 (Book I
                  heading at line 416, Book II from line 3084, Book III
                  from line 5062, Book IV from line 6708) through line
                  9006, ending "...tate, disserui." The Enchiridion ad
                  Laurentium, a second, distinct work bound in the same
                  volume -- NOT part of De Doctrina Christiana itself
                  -- begins at line 9019 (its own title block, "S.
                  AURELII AUGUSTINI"), with its own heading "ENCHIRIDION
                  AD LAURENTIUM" at line 9023 and its first line of
                  text ("C. 1. Uici non potest, dilectissime fili
                  Laurenti...") at line 9032, running to its own final
                  chapter (C. CXXII) around line 12812. A citing session
                  should use these line numbers to quote from the
                  correct work rather than treat the whole file as one
                  undifferentiated text. OCR quality is mediocre
                  (frequent u/n confusions, e.g. "Uici" for the
                  drop-cap "D" opening the Enchiridion's own first
                  word).

C.  II.  Omnis  doctrina  vel  rerum  est,  vel  signo- 
rum;  sed  res  per  signa  discuntur.  Proprie  autem 
nunc  res  adpellavi,  quae  non  ad  significandum  ali- 
quidadhibentur,  sicuti  est  lignum,  lapis,pecus,  atque

dine  carerent  ^);  neque  ille  lapis,  quem  lacob  sibi  ad 
caput  posuerat  ^);  neque  illud  pecus,  quod  pro  filio 
immolavit  Abraham.*)  llae  namque  ita  res  sunt, 
ut  aliarum  etiam  signa  sint  rerum.  Sunt  autem  alia 
signa,  qiiorum  omnis  usus  in  significando  est,  sicuti 
sunt  verba.  Nemo  enim  utitur  verbis  nisi  aliquid 
significandi  gratia.  Ex  quo  intelligitur,  quid  adpel- 
lem  signa;  res  eas  videlicet,  quae  ad  significandum 
aliquid  adhibentur.  Quam  ob  rem  omne  signum 
etiam  res  aliqua  est.  Quod  enim  nulla  res  est,  omnino 
nihil  est.  \on  autem  omnis  res  etiam  signum  est.

de  rebus  loquemur,  ita  loquemur,  ut,  etiam  si  earum

C.  III.  Res  ergo  aliae  sunt,  quibus  fruendum  est; 
aliae,  quibus  utendum;  aliae,  quae  fruuntur  et  utun- 
tur.  lllae,  quibus  fruendum  est,  beatos  nos  faciunt.

nos  beatos  faciunt  pervenire,  atque  his  inhaerere  pos-

riorum  amore  praepediti.
